title: "Reactions to the 2026 Iran war"
---

# Reactions to the 2026 Iran war

For reactions to the assassination of Iran's head of state during the war, see [Reactions to the assassination of Ali Khamenei](82615016.md).



**Government reactions to the 2026 Iran war:**&#x20;

&#x20; Iran

&#x20; Israel and the United States

&#x20; Countries that supported the Israeli-American attacks

&#x20; Countries that condemned the Iranian attacks

&#x20; Countries that condemned both attacks

&#x20; Countries that condemned the Israeli-American attacks

&#x20; Countries that expressed mixed reactions or made calls for peace

&#x20; No government reaction

On 28 February 2026, the [United States](3434750.md) and [Israel](9282173.md) launched joint airstrikes on [Iran](14653.md), starting the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md). The strikes targeted Iranian officials, military commanders, and military assets, [assassinating Ali Khamenei](82537558.md), the [supreme leader of Iran](36814344.md). Iran responded with missile and [drone strikes against Israel](82636370.md), U.S. bases, and [Arab countries](82751620.md) in the [Middle East](19323.md), as well as [closing the Strait of Hormuz](82550763.md).

Internationally, the war elicited reactions from governments, faith leaders and global governance bodies, as well as protests, unrest, and coverage from global media. Most countries did not take a definitive side in the conflict, but more countries opposed the US-Israeli attacks than supported them. Some US allies and partners, such as Canada, Saudi Arabia, Ukraine and Argentina, supported the attack, while others, such as France, Italy, Brazil and Turkey, expressed opposition. In the Middle East, most countries criticized the Iranian attacks. In Europe and Latin America, many countries condemned the US-Israeli attacks, and most condemned the Iranian retaliation. In Asia, many condemned the attacks or called for peace. The war provoked protests against it, as well as others in support of the [Iranian opposition](80207658.md).&#x20;

The war was unpopular in the United States, and increasingly so as it continued.[^cite_note-abc-polls-iran-1][^cite_note-\:6-2][^cite_note-\:1-3] A majority of Americans thought the decision to start the war was wrong,[^cite_note-\:7-4][^cite_note-\:8-5][^cite_note-\:6-2] that the war had hurt US interests,[^cite_note-\:9-6][^cite_note-\:10-7][^cite_note-\:12-8] and that it should end as soon as possible.[^cite_note-\:7-4][^cite_note-\:11-9][^cite_note-\:13-10] By July, 58% of Americans opposed the war,[^cite_note-\:1-3] and approval for Donald Trump had declined to an all-time low.[^cite_note-\:14-11][^cite_note-\:15-12][^cite_note-\:16-13] The [March 2026 No Kings protests](82478314.md) were the largest civilian protests in American history,[^cite_note-\:17-14] and *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* named the war "America's least popular since polls began".[^cite_note-\:18-15] Congress was divided on the issue of a [War Powers Resolution](209756.md#Iran,_2026), with the House in favor and the Senate opposed.[^cite_note-16] More Americans thought the war was [illegal under US and international law](82932609.md#Legality) than legal.[^cite_note-\:10-7][^cite_note-\:19-17]

Public opinion was opposed to the war in 30 countries across Europe, Asia and the Americas (including the US). Opposition to the war was 81% on average.[^cite_note-\:2-18] The war led to a worldwide decline in the reputation of the United States and Israel,[^cite_note-\:2-18][^cite_note-\:3-19][^cite_note-auto-20] with the US reputation falling behind that of China in most countries.[^cite_note-\:3-19][^cite_note-\:4-21] Israel was the only surveyed country where public opinion supported the war.[^cite_note-\:2-18]

Opinion on the attacks was divided within Iran, with many Iranians mourning casualties and attending large public rallies in support of the government. Millions of Iranians and Iraqis and many international leaders and dignitaries attended the [state funeral of Ali Khamenei](82570060.md). Others celebrated the attacks and expressed hope for [regime change](154074.md), particularly those within or affiliated with the exiled opposition. An anti-government uprising hoped for by the United States and Israel did not materialize, with some analysts saying that the attacks had bolstered the government's domestic popularity.

## Public opinion

### United States

See also: [Protests against the 2026 Iran war in the United States](82544763.md)



Protestors speak out against the US government's involvement in the war in [Portland, Oregon](23503.md), during [No Kings 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_No_Kings_protests). 28 March 2026

Opinion polls showed that most Americans opposed the war from the outset.[^cite_note-abc-polls-iran-1][^cite_note-\:6-2][^cite_note-\:1-3] Support for the war declined from 40% on March 3 to 34% by July 18, according to polling aggregates, with the share of Americans opposed to the war increasing from 48% to 58% over the same period.[^cite_note-\:1-3] Majorities of Americans thought that it was important to prevent Iran obtaining a nuclear weapon,[^cite_note-22] but said that it was the wrong decision to start the war,[^cite_note-\:7-4][^cite_note-\:8-5][^cite_note-\:6-2] that the war was not worth fighting,[^cite_note-23][^cite_note-auto1-24][^cite_note-25] that the war had hurt US interests,[^cite_note-\:9-6][^cite_note-\:10-7][^cite_note-\:12-8] that the US should end its attacks,[^cite_note-\:13-10][^cite_note-\:11-9] and that the war should end as soon as possible.[^cite_note-\:7-4][^cite_note-\:11-9] A plurality of Americans said that the war [was illegal](82932609.md#Legality) under both US and international law.[^cite_note-\:10-7][^cite_note-\:19-17]

In part a reaction to the war, the [March 2026 No Kings protests](82478314.md) were the largest civilian protests in American history.[^cite_note-\:17-14] In July, *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* named the Iran war "America's least popular since polls began".[^cite_note-\:18-15] Approval for Donald Trump declined to a record low in July,[^cite_note-\:14-11][^cite_note-\:15-12][^cite_note-26] with 37% approval and 60% disapproval by the end of September.[^cite_note-\:16-13][^cite_note-27] Trump's foreign affairs approval declined from 39% in January to 29% in September, with approval for his handling of Iran at 26%.[^cite_note-28] Opposition to the war remained high, with net support declining from -8% on March 3 to an all-time low of -27% on September 22.[^cite_note-\:1-3]

A March 1 Reuters/Ipsos poll found that 43% of Americans opposed the war, 27% were supportive and 29% were uncertain.[^cite_note-29] An NBC poll found 52% of registered voters were against military action, while 41% supported it.[^cite_note-30] Polls showed splits along political and demographic lines. Most Republicans supported the action, while most Democrats and independents opposed it.[^cite_note-abc-polls-iran-1]

Most Americans believed that Trump had not properly explained the war's goals.[^cite_note-31] A 22 March [CBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS) poll of 3,335 American adults reported that 92% believed it was important for the conflict to end as quickly as possible, four in five said it was important to make sure the Iranian people were safe and free, 73% said it was important for the US to permanently end the Iranian nuclear program, 68% said it was important to stop Iran from posing a threat to other countries, and 53% said it would be unacceptable to end the war with the Islamic Republic still in power.[^cite_note-jpost-polls-iran-32] A Reuters/Ipsos poll conducted from 17 to 19 March 2026, found that 65% thought Trump would order a large-scale ground war in Iran, while only 7% supported such a move.[^cite_note-Lange-33]

In an April 17–20 *Economist*-[YouGov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouGov) poll, more Americans agreed with [Pope Leo XIV's comments against the war](54520372.md#US_politics), by 48% to 28%, than with Trump and Vance. Atheists and agnostics were twice as likely to agree with him (80%) as Catholics or Protestants (42% and 39%, respectively).[^cite_note-34]

In a July 8–13 *[Washington Post](102226.md)*-[Ipsos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipsos) poll, 68% of Americans said the war had not been worth fighting, while 28% said it had. This assessment was worse than any *Post* poll during the 2003–2011 [Iraq War](5043324.md), and equalling the worst on Afghanistan in July 2013. According to *Post* polling, strong approval for Trump dropped from 27% in February to 15% in July, and net approval from 45% to 37%, the first time across both Trump terms that a majority of approvers support him only "somewhat" rather than "strongly".[^cite_note-auto1-24]

A July 12–15 *[Politico](9172097.md)* poll showed declining support for the war among Republican and MAGA voters.[^cite_note-35][^cite_note-36] Between May and July, Republicans in favor of continuing the war even if it increases costs for Americans declined from 46% to 32%, while those in favor only if it does not increased from 28% to 37%, and those against the war under all circumstances increased from 14% to 21%. Among MAGA voters, the shares were 37%, 37% and 19%, respectively.[^cite_note-37]

According to a July 10–13 *Economist*-YouGov poll, 55% think the US should not continue attacking Iran, and 27% think it should.[^cite_note-\:11-9] 65% against 15% wanted to end the war "as quickly as possible", including 82% of Democrats (against 5%), 66% of independents (against 11%), and 47% of Republicans (against 29%).[^cite_note-38]

In a July 17–19 [University of Maryland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Maryland,_College_Park) poll, a majority of Americans (58% to 12%) thought the war had a more negative than positive impact on American interests. Pluralities of Americans believed the US and Israel had initiated the war illegally under international law (by 39% to 19%) and under US law (40% to 25%). 22% said the US should focus on making the Middle East entirely nuclear-free (including both Israel, which has already [acquired nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_weapons_of_Israel), and Iran), 21% on preventing Iran acquiring nuclear weapons by force if necessary, and 19% on preventing it without military force, while 10% said that a nuclear-armed Israel and Iran could deter each other.[^cite_note-\:10-7]

In a July 20–24 [CBS News](359551.md)/YouGov poll, 76% of Americans believed the war had been more challenging than the Trump administration anticipated.[^cite_note-\:13-10]

The [Center on Conscience & War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_on_Conscience_&_War) reported that hotline callers opposed the [Minab school attack](82536519.md) and "dying for Israel".[^cite_note-39][^cite_note-40] A recent Reuters/Ipsos poll of 4,505 Americans, taken five months into the U.S. war with Iran, shows that only 35% support the conflict, while 58% expect gasoline prices to rise compared to just 15% who anticipate improvement.[^cite_note-41]

Two-third of American adults believe the war was not worth it.[^cite_note-42]

A [CNN](62028.md) poll on 2 March showed that 59% of Americans opposed the U.S. military action against Iran while 41% supported it.[^cite_note-43][^cite_note-44] A [Reuters](18998750.md)/[Ipsos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipsos) poll found that 47% disapprove of the strikes while 27% approve of them.[^cite_note-45][^cite_note-46] A [CBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS) poll published on 22 March, which questioned 3,335 American adults, reported that 92% believe it's important for the conflict to end as quickly as possible, four in five said it was important to make sure the Iranian people were safe and free, 73% said it was important for the US to permanently end the Islamic Republic's nuclear program, 68% said it was important to stop Iran from posing a threat to other countries, and 53% said it would be unacceptable to end the war with the Islamic Republic still in power.[^cite_note-jpost-polls-iran-32]

A Reuters/Ipsos poll conducted in March 2026 found that 65% of Americans believed President Donald Trump would order a large-scale ground war in Iran, though only 7% of respondents supported such a move.[^cite_note-Lange-33]

An Associated Press-NORC poll conducted in July 2026 found that 64% of Americans did not see the war with Iran as worth fighting while 33% did.[^cite_note-47]

#### Iranian Americans

A poll conducted by the [National Iranian American Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Iranian_American_Council), regarded as the Islamic Republic's lobby, found that 49.3% of [Iranian-American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian-American) respondents opposed the US-Israeli attack on Iran, while 48.9% supported it. A total of 61.6% supported diplomacy, while 35.4% opposed.[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-49] A [Zogby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Zogby) poll conducted from 24 to 27 March 2026 found that 66.1% of [Iranian Americans](2234641.md) opposed the war.[^cite_note-50]

### Iran

See also: [2025–2026 Iranian protests § After the strikes](81976180.md#After_the_strikes)



Worshippers gather at the [Grand Mosalla](62876839.md) for the first [Friday prayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friday_prayer) after the 2026 airstrikes on Tehran.



Demonstrators gather in Tehran for the annual International [Quds Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quds_Day) rally on 13 March 2026



Funeral procession for victims of the [airstrike on the Minab girls' school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Minab_school_airstrike), Iran, 3 March 2026

A holistic picture of civilian reactions in Iran is difficult to obtain due to the [government's control over the circulation of information](4297092.md), the [renewed internet blackout](82063825.md#Renewed_blackout), and the ongoing heated nature of the war.[^cite_note-51] After the initial strikes, both celebration and mourning at the killing of Iranian leaders was reported.[^cite_note-Bloomberg_News-2026-52][^cite_note-day-53][^cite_note-Madadi-2026-54][^cite_note-Gas-55][^cite_note-Photos-56][^cite_note-57] No anti-government uprising followed.[^cite_note-Bloomberg_News-2026-52]

Civilians expressed fear, grief and anger at the US–Israeli strikes, and at ensuing civilian casualties.[^cite_note-58][^cite_note-59] BBC reported that most Iranians consider attacking civilian infrastructure unacceptable.[^cite_note-60] Some civilians who had opposed the Islamic Republic and supported regime change later recoiled at the prolonged and violent nature of the war. A sociologist inside Iran said that the war was causing a [rally around the flag effect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rally_around_the_flag_effect).[^cite_note-61]

Early pro-government rallies occurred in [Yasuj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasuj), where crowds mourned Khamenei,[^cite_note-62] and in [Enqelab Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enqelab_Square) on 1 March.[^cite_note-63][^cite_note-Gas-55][^cite_note-Photos-56] Others celebrated the attacks, expressing the hope that the events might weaken or topple the regime.[^cite_note-64][^cite_note-day-53][^cite_note-Madadi-2026-54][^cite_note-65] Some celebrated the death of Khamenei.[^cite_note-66][^cite_note-67] A 1 March 2026 video showed a crowd toppling a monument dedicated to [Ruhollah Khomeini](26232.md).[^cite_note-68][^cite_note-69]

After [Mojtaba Khamenei](23324096.md) was named the next supreme leader, some Iranians chanted "Death to Mojtaba" from their windows in Tehran.[^cite_note-70][^cite_note-71]

As of 15 March, only pro-government rallies appeared in Tehran's major squares.[^cite_note-72] Many pro-government rallies followed, encouraged by authorities.[^cite_note-73] According to BBC, no mass anti-government rallies took place, in part due to police warnings of consequences for "tak\[-ing] action in the cities at the behest of the enemy".[^cite_note-74]

During the 17 March [Chaharshanbe Suri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaharshanbe_Suri) festivities ahead of the [Nowruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nowruz) holiday, many Iranians defied orders from the authorities and celebrated in the streets. Footage showed security forces firing gunshots to disperse crowds.[^cite_note-75][^cite_note-76]

On the last Friday of [Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramadan), thousands of Iranians took to the streets of Tehran in support of Palestinians and condemning the attacks as part of [Quds Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quds_Day) day. Many Iranian government figures were seen at the demonstrations, including Pezeshkian and [Ali Larijani](531848.md).[^cite_note-77] Israel threatened to attack the area, but demonstrations still took place.[^cite_note-78]

On 18 March, thousands of civilians joined a funeral procession for Larijani.[^cite_note-79] Residents also cheered from their homes and celebrated with fireworks after Larijani's death.[^cite_note-80][^cite_note-81]

Iran closed historic sites during [Nowruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nowruz) on 20 March, stating that ceremonies would not be held in these locations.[^cite_note-82] During [Nowruz celebrations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nowruz), activists affiliated with the [People's Mojahedin Organization of Iran](285632.md) (PMOI) Resistance Units conducted a campaign in [Zahedan](1044089.md), expressing support for the [National Council of Resistance of Iran](1181356.md) (NCRI) and its proposed provisional government.[^cite_note-83]

On 29 March, a resident was filmed saying "Thank you Trump" outside a police station in the [Chitgar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chitgar) destroyed by American airstrikes.[^cite_note-84] Trump's 1 April post threatening to send Iran "back to the Stone Age", received negative reactions from Iranians.[^cite_note-85] The same day, NPR reported that most Iranians fleeing to Turkey supported the US-Israeli strikes.[^cite_note-86][^cite_note-87]

### Israel

A March 2026 survey by the [Israel Democracy Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Democracy_Institute) (IDI) found that 82% of the Israeli public supported ongoing military operations, including 93% of [Jewish Israelis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Jews) and 26% of [Arab Israelis](492331.md).[^cite_note-timesofisrael-poll-88]

In a March 20–April 3 [Ipsos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipsos) survey, 40% of Israelis believed the war would end within the next month, a further 40% within three months, 14% by the end of the year, and 6% thought it would continue beyond the end of the year.[^cite_note-\:5-89]

A June 2026 survey by the [Hebrew University of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_University_of_Jerusalem) and Agam Institute found that 93.1% of Israelis believed that Iran had won the war, 82.9% believed it weakened Israel's long-term security, and 86% had a negative opinion of the [June peace agreement](83441543.md).[^cite_note-90]

### International



Share of each country opposed to involvement in the war. Majorities opposed in shades of red and majorities in support in blue.

In a March 20–April 3 [Ipsos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipsos) survey of 31 countries, a majority in 30 countries said that their country should avoid becoming involved in the Iran war, including large majorities in France, Germany, the UK, Australia, Canada, Argentina, South Korea and Japan, and in the US itself. Israel was the only country where a majority supported involvement in the war. On average, 81% of people opposed involvement.[^cite_note-\:2-18]

Across 30 countries in the Ipsos survey, the average share that believe that the US will have a positive impact on world affairs over the next decade was 38%, lower than for China at 50%. The average was 40% in the Asia-Pacific region, 28% in Europe, 56% in Latin America, and 42% in North America, and was lower than for China in all four regions.[^cite_note-\:2-18] The share fell in 27 out of 29 countries surveyed since October 2025. This deepened a decline seen during both Trump terms. Across 30 countries, the average share with a positive view of the US fell from 66% in October 2016 to 38% in April 2026 (having recovered to 64% in October 2022 under President Joe Biden).[^cite_note-\:5-89]

A Spring 2026 global study by [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center) found that the United States was viewed less positively than China in 25 out of 36 surveyed countries,[^cite_note-\:3-19] the first time in roughly 20 years of tracking that a majority of countries viewed China more positively.[^cite_note-\:4-21] Laura Silver, associate director of Pew's Global Attitudes Research, attributed the decline in trust to the war with Iran, as well as to the Trump administration's [threats to Greenland](82044223.md), the [intervention in Venezuela](82021123.md), and the US role in the [Gaza war](74998519.md).[^cite_note-\:4-21] These countries included traditional American allies such as France, Germany, the UK and Canada, and five out of six Latin American countries. Only six countries viewed the US more positively.[^cite_note-91][^cite_note-\:3-19][^cite_note-92]

The Pew study found that since 2025, opinions of Israel had declined in 13 out of 24 countries, improving in only one (Greece). The largest declines were in South Korea, Italy, Germany, Argentina and Nigeria. Views of [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) also declined in 13 out of 24 countries.[^cite_note-auto-20]

#### Argentina

A March 2026 survey by Zuban Córdoba, an agency in Argentina, showed that 7 out of 10 Argentines disapproved of the country's support for the US and Israel, with 66.4% of those surveyed responding that President Milei's alignment with those two countries is not representative of the [Argentine people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentine_people).[^cite_note-93][^cite_note-94] Another poll conducted by Zuban Córdoba in August 2026 about generalities of [Milei's foreign policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Javier_Milei) found a similar result, with only 26% of voters supporting the alignment with the United States, mostly from hardcore [libertarians](70967840.md).[^cite_note-95]

#### Australia

A March 2026 poll found that the majority of Australians (61%) wanted their country to "stay out of the conflict entirely" and only 13% wanted Australia to become involved. 39% of respondents opposed US-Israeli military actions in Iran while 28% supported them. At the same time, 47% supported regime change in Tehran, compared to 9% wanting the current government to stay in power.[^cite_note-96]

#### Canada

A March [Angus Reid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipsos-Reid) 2026 poll found that 48% of Canadians opposed the air strikes against Iran, while 34% supported them.[^cite_note-97]

#### Germany

A March 2026 [Infratest-dimap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infratest_dimap) poll found that 58% of Germans said US attacks on Iran were not justified.[^cite_note-98]

#### Italy

A poll found that 56% opposed the US and Israel's attacks against Iran.[^cite_note-aa-99]

#### Japan

A poll published by the [Jiji Press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jiji_Press) on 12 March found that only 7% of Japanese supported the US-Israeli attacks on Iran, while 75% did not support them. Broken down by party, those who opposed were measured at 69.9% by [Liberal Democratic Party](164231.md) supporters, 71.4% by [Japan Innovation Party](48459506.md) supporters, 83.3% by [Centrist Reform Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centrist_Reform_Alliance) supporters, 75.0% by [Democratic Party for the People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_For_the_People) supporters, and 71.3% for supporters of [Sanae Takaichi](8125532.md)'s Cabinet.[^cite_note-100] According to an ANN opinion poll conducted on 21 and 22 March, 7% supported the US and Israel's attacks while 86% did not support them, and 52% opposed dispatching the [Japan Self-Defense Forces](2785204.md) to ensure security in the region.[^cite_note-101]

A [Kyodo News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyodo_News) poll released on 8 March found that 50% of Japanese supported their government's response to the US-Israeli attack while 42.9% did not support it.[^cite_note-102] According to a Kyodo News poll conducted on 4 and 5 April, 80.3% said the US-Israeli attack on Iran was a "mistaken decision". At the same time, 50.1% supported the response of their government in the war while 42.2% did not support it.[^cite_note-Nikkei-2026-103]

#### Saudi Arabia

A June 2026 poll found that 49% of respondents supported Saudi strikes on Iranian missile launch sites.[^cite_note-104]

#### Spain

A poll found that 68% of respondents rejected the US and Israel's attacks on Iran.[^cite_note-aa-99]

#### United Kingdom

A 2 March 2026 [YouGov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouGov) poll found that Britons opposed the attacks by 49% to 28%. A 10 March 2026 poll found that 59% opposed.[^cite_note-105]

## Protests and unrest

Main article: [Protests against the 2026 Iran war](82544763.md)

### North America

Main article: [Protests against the 2026 Iran war in the United States](82544763.md)



Anti-war protestors in Washington D.C.



Iranian diaspora members protesting in Washington, D.C., following the military strikes on Iran on 28 February 2026

Several "Hands Off Iran" protests were organized in the United States. Approximately 100 people attended a rally at Federal Plaza in [Chicago](6886.md).[^cite_note-106][^cite_note-107] In [Columbus, Ohio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columbus,_Ohio), a protest was held in [Goodale Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goodale_Park).[^cite_note-108] In [Indianapolis](57707.md), a protest was held at Military Park.[^cite_note-109] Indivisible Jax organized a protest in [Jacksonville, Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacksonville,_Florida).[^cite_note-110] Hundreds of people gathered at [Times Square](31389.md) in [New York City](645042.md).[^cite_note-111][^cite_note-112] Approximately 100 people gathered at [Pioneer Courthouse Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pioneer_Courthouse_Square) in [downtown Portland, Oregon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Portland,_Oregon).[^cite_note-113][^cite_note-114][^cite_note-115] In [Seattle](11388236.md), anti-war protesters gathered at [Pike Place Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pike_Place_Market).[^cite_note-116]

On 1 March, a [shooting at a bar in Austin, Texas](82545863.md), was carried out by an American man of [Senegalese](27278.md) descent wearing a shirt that read "Property of Allah" and an undershirt bearing the flag of the Islamic Republic of Iran.[^cite_note-117] The same day, a local gym owned by an Iranian-Canadian anti-regime activist located outside [Toronto](64646.md) was [shot with 17 bullets](82164645.md#Violence_after_28_February_strikes), hours after [Khamenei's death](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khamenei's_death).[^cite_note-118][^cite_note-119][^cite_note-120] On 10 March, two suspects fired multiple shots at the U.S. Consulate in [Toronto](64646.md), an act the [Royal Canadian Mounted Police](69080.md) designated a "national security incident." Prime Minister Mark Carney condemned the shooting as "reprehensible violence," while Ontario Premier [Doug Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doug_Ford) called the attack "absolutely unacceptable." In response, the RCMP heightened security at U.S. and Israeli diplomatic missions in Toronto and [Ottawa](22219.md).[^cite_note-121][^cite_note-122]

### Asia

[Gulf Arab](3088555.md) businessmen, like Emirati billionaire [Khalaf al-Habtoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalaf_al-Habtoor?action=edit\&redlink=1), sent protests to Donald Trump for dragging the region into war with Iran without any consultation and questioning if the US calculated the risks for the Arab countries security and if their military activity was an initiative from the [Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump_admin_2) or by pressures from the State of Israel and its own agenda in the context of [Arab–Israeli conflict](7960202.md).[^cite_note-123]

In March, Japanese organization [Nihon Hidankyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nihon_Hidankyo) issued a statement calling for a cease-fire and accusing the United States and Israel of carrying out "preemptive strikes on Iran that disregard international law."[^cite_note-124]

In [Baghdad](4492.md), demonstrators reacting to Khamenei's assassination confronted security forces near the [Green Zone](856542.md), and protesters attempted to storm the [US embassy](4754335.md) and block roads leading to it. Protesters were seen waving flags and shouting slogans while mourning Khamenei.[^cite_note-125]

[Riots broke out near the US consulate](82542761.md) in [Karachi](17123.md) in response to the assassination, with demonstrators burning and smearing the building's windows and attempting to storm it. As protests escalated, U.S. [Marine Security Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine_Security_Guard) personnel at the consulate in Karachi opened fire, killing at least 10 protesters and injuring over 60 people.[^cite_note-Jawad-126][^cite_note-127] Demonstrators also vandalized several other buildings in Pakistan, including a regional office affiliated with the [UNMOGIP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Military_Observer_Group_in_India_and_Pakistan) in [Skardu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skardu), [Gilgit-Baltistan](18807336.md).[^cite_note-128][^cite_note-Jawad-126]

There have also been protests and violent unrest in Bahrain by the Shiite majority, directed against the ruling Sunni [Al-Khalifa dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Khalifa) and the United States. Bahraini authorities have arrested people for celebrating Iranian strikes on sites in Bahrain or posting footage of Iranian strikes online.[^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130][^cite_note-131]

### Europe



Protesters at Burgplatz, [Düsseldorf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D%C3%BCsseldorf), on 1 March 2026



Placard in Paris reading "[Women Life Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woman,_Life,_Freedom) means no to war" in Persian, French and English

On 8 March, an explosion was reported at the [Embassy of the United States in Oslo](16815581.md), [Norway](21241.md).[^cite_note-132][^cite_note-133] On 9 March, an explosion was reported in [Liège](28341707.md), [Belgium](3343.md), where it caused damage to a synagogue.[^cite_note-134]

### Betting

There has been betting on events and the outcome of the war, prompting US politicians to call for a ban on such speculation, raising questions about whether the bets were based on insider information and what betting markets should be allowed. Notably, [Polymarket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polymarket) has been used extensively, with betters making large winnings on various activities during the early days of the war.[^cite_note-135]

## Iran

Government and military leaders uniformly decried the attacks.[^cite_note-Khalil-136] The public divided into supporters and opponents of attacks, although security forces quickly suppressed the former.[^cite_note-Bloomberg_News-2026-52][^cite_note-Madadi-2026-54] Religious leaders also opposed the attacks, defending the regime.[^cite_note-jihad-137] The Iranian diaspora generally supported the attacks, anticipating regime change.[^cite_note-happeningfinally-138]

### Government

Iran's Foreign Ministry vowed a response as Iranian forces struck US bases across the [Persian Gulf](24761.md).[^cite_note-139][^cite_note-140] The [Supreme National Security Council](6295948.md) said Iran was targeted by a "brutal air operation" carried out by the US and Israel, saying: "This occurred once again during negotiations, and the enemy imagines that the resilient Iranian nation will surrender to their petty demands through these cowardly actions."[^cite_note-141] Iranian foreign minister [Abbas Araghchi](21951274.md) called the attacks "wholly unprovoked, illegal, and illegitimate" and said attacks by Iran will continue until as long as they are defending.[^cite_note-142][^cite_note-Khalil-136] Vice President Aref stated that the path to social justice will not be resisted or stopped by any missile attack.[^cite_note-143] Iranian president [Masoud Pezeshkian](2650383.md) said that if the US and Israel are not held accountable for their actions "global order will be shaken".[^cite_note-144] Pezeshkian said that US should take their unconditional surrender demand "to their grave".[^cite_note-The_Indian_Express-2026-145] He initially apologized for the attacks on neighboring nations, blaming "miscommunication in the ranks".[^cite_note-The_Indian_Express-2026-145][^cite_note-146] Later he said that Iran had to target countries whose territory was used to attack Iran.[^cite_note-147] Iranian officials rejected Trump's attempt to insert himself into the [government's succession plans](59042456.md).[^cite_note-148]

On 19 March, Iranian [Foreign Ministry spokesperson](51080171.md) [Esmail Baghaei](81031766.md) accused the US and Israel of "terrorist acts" over the deaths of top figures, calling the attacks an "illegal war". He said that Australia's military assets were legitimate targets, over the deployment of a [surveillance aircraft](46818.md) along with personnel to the UAE.[^cite_note-149][^cite_note-150] On 20 March, [Abolfazl Shekarchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abolfazl_Shekarchi) warned US and Israeli personnel that Iran was tracking the movements of "officials, commanders, and pilots" beyond active conflict zones and warned that public and recreational locations would no longer be safe for them.[^cite_note-151] Iran denied targeting Azerbaijan, Turkey and Oman, blaming "Israeli [false flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_flag)" attacks.[^cite_note-152][^cite_note-153]

Vice President [Mohammad Reza Aref](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Reza_Aref) described the alleged use of a [bunker-buster bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bunker-buster_bomb) in an attack on the university as a "symbol of Trump’s madness and ignorance".[^cite_note-154] Deputy foreign minister [Kazem Gharibabadi](78777113.md) condemned what he called the repeated [targeting of historical and cultural sites in Iran](82533582.md#Cultural_heritage_sites) as "an assault on part of Iran's civilizational identity".[^cite_note-155] Araghchi labeled the [strike on a Tehran fuel depot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_rain#Notable_incidences) as "ecocide".[^cite_note-156] The Iranian government declared it had lifted import/export rules and restrictions,[^cite_note-157] and announced that fuel and medicine were readily available.[^cite_note-158] The [national post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Post_Company_of_the_Islamic_Republic_of_Iran) temporarily paused parcel delivery.[^cite_note-159] All sporting events were canceled. Iranian [hajj](23487999.md) pilgrims returned by land.[^cite_note-160] On 13 June, Iranian hardliners chanted against Araghchi and Parliament Speaker and chief negotiator [Mohammad Bagher Ghalibaf](1421458.md) as they protested [the deal](83441543.md) with the United States outside the [Iran Foreign Ministry](1166971.md) office.[^cite_note-161]

Tehran alleged that the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) (UAE) played an active role in the war from its inception, stating that the UAE had acted in addition to hosting US facilities.[^cite_note-162] Iranian ambassador to the UN [Amir-Saeid Iravani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amir-Saeid_Iravani) demanded compensation from Bahrain, Jordan, Qatar, Saudi Arabia, and the UAE for allegedly violating their obligations towards Iran.[^cite_note-163]

The SNSC ordered people to evacuate [Tehran](57654.md), but not [Chabahar](1658958.md), [Bandar Abbas](464348.md), [Mahshahr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mahshahr), and [Bushehr](205669.md).[^cite_note-164] Schools closed and students switched to offline home [Shaadapp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaad).[^cite_note-165] The government also renewed a "near total" [internet blackout in Iran](82063825.md), as [NetBlocks](59736785.md) reported internet activity dropping to 4% of ordinary levels.[^cite_note-166][^cite_note-167][^cite_note-168] Iranians abroad reported that they were unable to reach family in Iran.[^cite_note-169] On 19 March, Iran promised more attacks on energy sites in the region.[^cite_note-170] They also threatened that if the US would attack their power grid, they would attack the US electricity infrastructure.[^cite_note-171] Iranian foreign minister [Abbas Araghchi](21951274.md) also condemned the attacks on Iranian nuclear facilities and asked compensation.[^cite_note-172]

On 29 March 2026, parliament speaker [Mohammad Bagher Ghalibaf](1421458.md) rejected negotiations with the United States.[^cite_note-173] Ghalibaf said that entities that hold US bonds are legitimate targets.[^cite_note-174] The IRGC threatened American and Israeli universities.[^cite_note-175] On 15 April, [Ali Nikzad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Nikzad) said that Iran would avenge the killing of Ali Khamenei and other top commanders.[^cite_note-176]

Iran has sued the US in the Hague Tribunal over the 2026 war, invoking the [Algiers Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algiers_Accords_\(1981\)).[^cite_note-177]

#### State funeral of Ali Khamenei

These paragraphs are an excerpt from [State funeral of Ali Khamenei](82570060.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=State_funeral_of_Ali_Khamenei\&action=edit)]

The [state funeral](714370.md) of [Ali Khamenei](385653.md), the second [supreme leader of Iran](36814344.md), took place from 3 to 9 July 2026, following [his assassination](82537558.md) in a US-Israeli attack on 28 February 2026.[^cite_note-178] Several ceremonies were held in [Tehran](57654.md) and [Qom](638742.md), alongside separate events in Iraq ([Najaf](200512.md) and [Karbala](210957.md)), before his burial at the [Imam Reza shrine](14030191.md) in [Mashhad](406512.md).

Major processions began on 3 July, with large rallies and foreign participants arriving.[^cite_note-State_funeral_of_Ali_Khamenei_\:4-179] Hundreds of thousands of followers attended the first day of the funeral, and the Iranian government facilitated the event.[^cite_note-State_funeral_of_Ali_Khamenei_auto1-180][^cite_note-State_funeral_of_Ali_Khamenei_auto3-181] The funeral procession in Tehran was attended by millions coming from all over Iran.[^cite_note-State_funeral_of_Ali_Khamenei_ft-182] The absence of [Mojtaba Khamenei](23324096.md), Iran's new supreme leader and Ali Khamenei's son, from the funeral received global coverage, raising questions about his health and as to who is leading the country.[^cite_note-State_funeral_of_Ali_Khamenei_\:0-183][^cite_note-184][^cite_note-185][^cite_note-186][^cite_note-187]

Crowds carried banners of the slogan of the funeral, "We must rise" ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): باید برخاست, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Persian): *Bâyad barxâst*), alongside a red clenched fist, symbolizing Iran's resistance against the United States and Israel.[^cite_note-State_funeral_of_Ali_Khamenei_guardian1-188][^cite_note-189] Mourners called for revenge, carrying banners threatening violence against [Donald Trump](4848272.md) and [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md), and chanting "[Death to America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_to_America)" and "[Death to Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_to_Israel)".[^cite_note-State_funeral_of_Ali_Khamenei_ft-182][^cite_note-190]

### Religious leaders

Ayatollahs [Naser Makarem Shirazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naser_Makarem_Shirazi) and [Hossein Noori Hamedani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hossein_Noori_Hamedani) issued a [fatwa](11259.md) for [jihad](16203.md) against America for Muslims to avenge Khamenei's death, saying vengeance was "the religious duty of all Muslims".[^cite_note-jihad-137]

### Diaspora

Further information: [2026 Iranian diaspora protests § Celebrations following the Israeli–United States strikes on Iran](82164645.md#Celebrations_following_the_Israeli%E2%80%93United_States_strikes_on_Iran), and [Iranian opposition](80207658.md)



Exiled Iranian opposition leader [Reza Pahlavi](613947.md) is aligned with Israel and the United States and has prepared a transitional government that he says is ready to take over should the Islamic Republic be overthrown.

[^cite_note-191]

[^cite_note-192]

Celebratory rallies were held worldwide by the [Iranian opposition](80207658.md),[^cite_note-193][^cite_note-Hague-194][^cite_note-TOI-195] especially in Europe and North America.[^cite_note-Hague-194][^cite_note-196][^cite_note-197][^cite_note-happeningfinally-138][^cite_note-TOI-195] Many [Iranians residing in Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranians_in_Japan) expressed support for regime change in public rallies or interviews with local media.[^cite_note-198][^cite_note-199] [Maryam Rajavi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maryam_Rajavi), leader of the France–Albania based [National Council of Resistance of Iran](1181356.md), the political wing of [People's Mojahedin Organization of Iran](285632.md), announced the formation of a rival transitional government and rejected both the Islamic Republic and the Iran Prosperity Project.[^cite_note-200]

[Reza Pahlavi](613947.md), the US-based exiled son of the former [Shah of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Reza_Shah), urged Iranians inside Iran to prepare to resume [protests](81976180.md) as the Islamic Republic "colapses", called on [military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_of_Iran) and security forces to side with the public over the government, and described US action against Iran as a "humanitarian intervention" while urging Trump to avoid civilian harm.[^cite_note-201] Pahlavi stated that he did not intend to seek political office or restore [the monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monarchism_in_Iran), but aimed instead to help guide Iran through a peaceful transition to a democratic system.[^cite_note-202]

## United States



US president Trump overseeing the start of "Operation Epic Fury"

See also: [Rationale for the 2026 Iran war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rationale_for_the_2026_Iran_war)

Reactions inside the US were overall negative but split along party lines, with the majority of Republicans supporting the war and the majority of Democratic and independent voters opposing it.[^cite_note-203][^cite_note-abc-polls-iran-1] Pundits split similarly, although some notable right-wing pundits such as [Matt Walsh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matt_Walsh_\(political_commentator\)) opposed it.[^cite_note-Griffing_2026-204]

### Government reactions to developments in the war

Trump wrote on 6 March: "There will be no deal with Iran except UNCONDITIONAL SURRENDER!"[^cite_note-205] On 9 March, Trump said that "the war is very complete, pretty much", and falsely claimed the Iranian military had been destroyed and the Strait of Hormuz had re-opened.[^cite_note-206] Five days later, he called for US allies and China to send warships to help re-open the strait.[^cite_note-207] Trump went on to claim that the US and Israel had "won" the war several times, while continuing to say that the US had not yet completed its objectives.[^cite_note-shifting_timeline-208][^cite_note-209] He denied that he had ever called for regime change.[^cite_note-210] The president also repeatedly claimed that Iran's military had been destroyed, despite it continuing to launch attacks.[^cite_note-211][^cite_note-212]

In late March and early April, Trump repeatedly gave ultimatums to Iran, threatening to destroy Iran's civilian infrastructure and bombing it "back to the Stone Ages" if it did not make a deal with the US and re-open the Hormuz strait.[^cite_note-213] On 7 April, the president threatened that "a whole civilization will die tonight".[^cite_note-214][^cite_note-215] Following the failed [Islamabad Talks](82923583.md), Trump said that he "doesn't care" about negotiations,[^cite_note-216] and announced that the US itself would impose a [naval blockade of Iran](82937256.md) from 13 April.[^cite_note-217] Trump said on 17 April that the Hormuz strait is "COMPLETELY OPEN",[^cite_note-shifting_timeline-208] but on 23 April Trump claimed that he is the one keeping it closed, because opening it would allow Iran to make "\$500 million a day".[^cite_note-218] In March 2026, Trump indicated that he and Director of National Intelligence [Tulsi Gabbard](26328774.md) differ on their approach to Iran and [its nuclear program](721807.md), labeling her as "softer" on the issue. In May 2026, Gabbard announced her resignation effective June 30, stating that she needs to care for her husband following his diagnosis with a rare form of bone cancer. According to a source familiar with her departure, she was compelled to leave by the White House.[^cite_note-219] In July 2026, US Vice President J.D Vance criticized conservative [hawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_hawk) in the US, stating that they want to topple the Iranian regime, despite the fact that US history with doing so has been negative and involves a significant commitment of ground troops.[^cite_note-220]

### Congress



Strike footage with computer game [points](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Score_\(video_games\)) added by the White House

American lawmakers had mixed reactions. Republican senator [Lindsey Graham](363279.md) supported the action, justifying the strikes as "in the nick of time" to prevent Iran from acquiring a nuclear weapon.[^cite_note-221] In a *[Fox News](11121.md)* interview, he said, "I'm going back to South Carolina, I am asking them to send their sons and daughters to the Mideast. What I want you to do in the Mideast, my friends in Saudi Arabia and other places, step forward and say this is my fight too."[^cite_note-222]

Other Republican Senators supported the attacks, including Senate majority leader [John Thune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Thune), as well as some Democrats, including Senator [John Fetterman](5303368.md) and Representative [Josh Gottheimer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josh_Gottheimer).[^cite_note-223] A few Republicans opposed the operation, including Representative [Thomas Massie](28005891.md) and former representative [Marjorie Taylor Greene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marjorie_Taylor_Greene).[^cite_note-224][^cite_note-225]

In the House, Speaker [Mike Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Johnson) said that Israel would have attacked Iran even without US help, with "devastating" consequences. Johnson called US involvement "absolutely necessary for our defense".[^cite_note-McCreesh-226] Similar declarations came from [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) affirming that the US knew that Israel was planning to attack and that the US acted to avoid higher casualties. [Critics of Israel](30074296.md) denounced this as US strategy that is increasingly shaped by Israeli military decisions.[^cite_note-227] Rubio said that the leadership is the problem and not the people. He said that the goals of the war were to degrade Iranian military capabilities and to make sure that they did not control the [Strait of Hormuz](29005.md).[^cite_note-228] Republican senator [Rand Paul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rand_Paul) denounced the attacks.[^cite_note-McCreesh-226]

Democratic politicians tended to be more withdrawn. Representative [Jim Himes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Himes) stated, "Everything I have heard from the administration before and after these strikes on Iran confirms this is a [war of choice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_aggression) with no strategic endgame."[^cite_note-Khan-2026-229] Senator [Mark Warner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Warner), after attending a classified briefing on the war, denied any immediate threat.[^cite_note-McCreesh-226] Senator [Tim Kaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Kaine) called for a check on Trump's power to engage in war without the support of Congress and was supported by Democratic [Minority Leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_leaders_of_the_United_States_House_of_Representatives) [Chuck Schumer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuck_Schumer) who said that the answers at the classified briefing were "completely and totally insufficient",[^cite_note-McCreesh-226] House minority leader [Hakeem Jeffries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakeem_Jeffries),[^cite_note-Khan-2026-229] and Democratic senator [Andy Kim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andy_Kim) concurred.[^cite_note-230]

Congress members introduced a [resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resolution_\(law\)) under the [War Powers Resolution](209756.md),[^cite_note-NBC_News-231] that would have stifled the war. Drafting began before the first strikes.[^cite_note-232] The resolution would have stopped US attacks after sixty days (with an extra thirty if Trump wrote to Congress) without a Congressional war declaration.[^cite_note-NBC_News-231] The resolutions failed in both Houses.[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234]

On 18 April, US congressman [Greg Steube](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greg_Steube) said that the US was in charge of the situation and would ensure that Iran does not get a nuclear weapon.[^cite_note-235]

As the US Defense Secretary Hegseth was asking the [Senate Appropriations Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_Appropriations_Committee) on 22 July for an urgent \$87 billion for the Pentagon, the hearing was interrupted many times by protests and culminated with a shouting match between Hegseth and Democratic senators.[^cite_note-236]

### Local officials

California governor [Gavin Newsom](418947.md) condemned the attacks, accusing Israel of dragging the United States into a long war.[^cite_note-canewsom-237][Northern Mariana Islands](21402.md) governor [David M. Apatang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_M._Apatang) praised Trump's actions and congratulated him for "doing what you needed to do", saying that he prayed for the safe return of US service members.[^cite_note-238]

### Pundits

&#x20;Some right-wing commentators condemned the operation. *[The Daily Wire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Daily_Wire)* host [Matt Walsh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matt_Walsh_\(political_commentator\)) called the Trump administration's messaging "confusing" and contradictory.[^cite_note-Griffing_2026-204] Walsh and [Andrew Tate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Tate) described armed conflict as irrelevant to the needs of the American people.[^cite_note-239]



Protester on 28 February 2026, in Washington D.C. promoting the theory that the war was a distraction from the [Jeffrey Epstein](6253522.md) investigation

Other Trump critics, including some social media influencers and users, claimed that the war was an attempt at distraction from the [Epstein files](80415168.md), satirically referring to the war as the "Epstein War" and "Operation Epstein Fury", the latter a play on "Operation Epic Fury".[^cite_note-240][^cite_note-241][^cite_note-242][^cite_note-243]

On 17 March, [Joe Kent](68079795.md) resigned as the director of the National Counterterrorism Center, citing policy disagreements with the Trump administration regarding the war in Iran.[^cite_note-244]

On April 5, *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* published an editorial in support of the strikes on Iran, characterizing them as a reasonable decision from Trump after failed diplomatic efforts and arguing that other options presented a risk of permitting Iran to develop nuclear weaponry.[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-246]

Former CIA analyst [Ken Pollack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenneth_M._Pollack) warned that the worst possible outcome could be if the war ended without toppling the Iranian government.[^cite_note-247]

## Israel

See also: [2026 Iranian strikes on Israel](82636370.md)



Israelis pay tribute to US troops at a memorial in [Dizengoff Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dizengoff_Square), Tel Aviv, following the 1 March 2026 drone strike in Kuwait

Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) was the first to report and celebrate the assassination, stating "for 47 years, the Ayatollah regime has chanted '[Death to Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_to_Israel)' and '[Death to America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_to_America).' It has spilled our blood, murdered many Americans, and slaughtered its own people."[^cite_note-248] Defense Minister [Israel Katz](19041378.md) celebrated the killing, stating "he who acted to destroy Israel – has been destroyed ... Justice has been served, and the [axis of evil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Axis_of_evil) has suffered a mortal blow."[^cite_note-Jazeera-249] On 29 March, [Yuli Edelstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuli_Edelstein) said that the strikes had set Iran back by years, but they had not yet succeeded in removing the Iranian threat.[^cite_note-250] Opposition leaders also supported the strikes.[^cite_note-251]

## International

See also: [Reactions to the assassination of Ali Khamenei § International](82615016.md#International)

The [IAEA](14984.md) Board of Governors convened an emergency meeting as requested by the [Russian Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Federation) on 2 March.[^cite_note-252] IAEA expressed "deep concern" amid attacks on Iranian nuclear facilities, including the [Bushehr Nuclear Power Plant](18522794.md).[^cite_note-253] The head of the [World Health Organization](33583.md) condemned the attacks. The secretary general of [Amnesty International](18947898.md) described such actions as potential war crimes.[^cite_note-254]

According to the founding chief prosecutor of the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) (ICC) [Luis Moreno Ocampo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Moreno_Ocampo), the war was comparable to [Russia's war in Ukraine](42085878.md), adding that the world is going from a "rules-based system" to the "rule of the man", which was "not a viable world".[^cite_note-255]

European Commission president ⁠[Ursula von der Leyen](2991932.md) and Council president [Antonio Costa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Costa) called the conflict "greatly concerning" and urged restraint.[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera_English-2026a-256] On 1 March 2026 Von der Leyen advocated a "credible transition" of power.[^cite_note-Europa-257][^cite_note-Zsiros-258] After Trump's threats to destroy Iranian infrastructure if the Strait of Hormuz was not reopened on 5 April,[^cite_note-259] Costa denounced "any targeting of civilian infrastructure, namely energy facilities" as illegal and called for a diplomatic solution only.[^cite_note-260]

A spokesperson stated that [NATO](21133.md) was closely watching, while a senior official stated that NATO's vigilance was heightened.[^cite_note-Korte-261][^cite_note-Bayer-262] NATO secretary general [Mark Rutte](4321056.md) said that Europe is supportive of the war, as Iran was a threat.[^cite_note-BBC-263] He also said that he felt that the US "knows what it is doing".[^cite_note-264]

Support for the Israeli–United States actions was voiced by countries such as [Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Argentina_in_the_2026_Iran_war), [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_and_the_2026_Iran_war),[^cite_note-265] [Canada](82593114.md),[^cite_note-Politico-266] [Ukraine](82720554.md), Germany,[^cite_note-Psaropoulos-267] Paraguay,[^cite_note-hORA-268] Papua New Guinea,[^cite_note-269] and Uganda,[^cite_note-jpostuganda-270] as well as various EU member states.[^cite_note-poleureactions-271] Other countries made less explicit statements of support.[^cite_note-272]

The war was condemned by [progressive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressivism)[^cite_note-273] and left-wing parties,[^cite_note-274][^cite_note-275] trade unions,[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279] as well as [anti-war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-war) organizations.[^cite_note-280][^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282] The [communist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism) parties of the [US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_USA), [Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tudeh_Party_of_Iran), and [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maki_\(political_party\)) issued a joint statement opposing the attack.[^cite_note-283] Some [far-right](221220.md) movements in the [European Conservatives and Reformists Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Conservatives_and_Reformists_Group) (such as [AfD](38708229.md), [Vlaams Belang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlaams_Belang), [ANO](40808475.md), [National Rally](67119.md), [Brothers of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brothers_of_Italy), etc.) criticized the war as being against [international law](8195726.md) and the [national interests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_interest) of European countries, while others (such as [Vox](41928488.md), [Reform UK](59757202.md), [Fidesz](404647.md), [Law and Justice](520309.md)) supported the actions in the name of the [free world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_World).[^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285]

After initially opposing war, on 16 March, Gulf countries urged the US to neutralise Iran due to Iranian strikes on their territories.[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287] On 19 March, several Arab states asked for a discussion in the [UN Human Rights Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Human_Rights_Council) to discuss Iran's strikes on civilians and energy infrastructure in the region.[^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289]

Iran's retaliations were condemned by Bahrain, Cyprus, Egypt, India, Kuwait, the Palestinian Authority, Qatar,[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera_English-2026a-256] Saudi Arabia,[^cite_note-290] Syria, Turkey,[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera_English-291] the UAE,[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera_English-2026a-256] several other EU countries,[^cite_note-poleureactions-271] as well as by France, Germany, and the [United Kingdom](82581844.md), who issued a joint declaration. The United Kingdom permitted the US to use British military bases for defensive purposes. Portugal permitted the US to use [Lajes Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lajes_Field) for defensive purposes,[^cite_note-292] and France sent its aircraft carrier towards the Mediterranean.[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera_English-291] Ireland,[^cite_note-293] Slovenia,[^cite_note-poleureactions-271] and Vatican City.[^cite_note-294] In addition to condemnations,[^cite_note-poleureactions-271] Spain denied the use of its military bases for US flights connected to the offensive.[^cite_note-295] On 20 March 2026, the UK approved the US to use British bases to attack Iranian missile sites targeting vessels in the Strait ‌of Hormuz.[^cite_note-296]

Canada [abstained from joining in US-led Israel war in Iran](82593114.md). On 5 March 2026, France authorized the use of French bases. Mixed reactions or calls for peace were made by South Africa,[^cite_note-Post-297] Azerbaijan,[^cite_note-oc-298] Lebanon.[^cite_note-Apa.az-299][^cite_note-LBCIV7-300][^cite_note-Al_Jazeera_English-2026a-256]

In Pakistan, the [army](1965925.md) was deployed to suppress an escalation of violence during [pro-Iranian protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro-Iranian_protests_during_the_2026_Israeli%E2%80%93United_States_strikes_on_Iran) in the [northern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Pakistan) cities of [Gilgit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilgit) and [Skardu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skardu), especially after Shiite demonstrators attacked on 1 March the offices of the [UN Military Observer Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Military_Observer_Group_for_India_and_Pakistan) at [Azad Kashmir](2745.md). Similar action was taken in [Sindh](28972.md) during the [attack on the US consulate in Karachi](82542761.md).[^cite_note-301] There have been movements between Saudi Arabia and Pakistan due to the [Strategic Mutual Defence Agreement](81115024.md) between both countries, as it "states that any aggression against either country shall be considered an aggression against both".

On 11 March 2026, the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) passed [Resolution 2817](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2817), sponsored by [Bahrain](18933277.md), which called for Iran to stop its attack on Gulf states and condemned the strikes as a violation of international law. The resolution did not mention US–Israel strikes on Iran and Lebanon.[^cite_note-302][^cite_note-303]

### States

This is a [dynamic list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:WikiProject_Lists#Dynamic_lists) and may never be able to satisfy particular standards for completeness. You can help by [editing the page](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:EditPage/Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war) to add missing items, with references to [reliable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Reliable_sources).

#### Africa

-  [Algeria](358.md): The Foreign Ministry condemned the attacks on Iran, calling them a "flagrant violation of sovereignty" and "an American-Zionist aggression."

  [^cite_note-fourafricannations-304]

  &#x20;Algeria later cosponsored [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2817](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2817), condemning Iran's retaliatory attacks on the Gulf states.

  [^cite_note-\:0-305]

-  [Angola](701.md): The Press Office of the President issued a statement stating that government is following the escalation with "deep concern" and called for "maximum restraint" and dialogue to cease hostilities.

  [^cite_note-306]

-  [Benin](3459.md): Benin cosponsored [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2817](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2817), condemning Iran's retaliatory attacks on the Gulf states.

  [^cite_note-\:0-305]

-  [Botswana](3464.md): The government issued a statement on 17 March denying rumors that Botswana was hosting U.S. forces in the Thebephatshwa Air Base in [Kweneng District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kweneng_District), saying that the [Botswana Defence Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Botswana_Defence_Force) is in full control of the base and that misinformation could put national security at risk.

  [^cite_note-AfricaBInsider-307]

  [^cite_note-308]

  &#x20;In the same statement, Botswana reaffirmed its commitment to regional cooperation, dialogue, and mutual respect, adding that it values good relationships with all its neighbors.

  [^cite_note-AfricaBInsider-307]

-  [Cape Verde](18962637.md): President [José Maria Neves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Maria_Neves) said that Cape Verde has always appealed for the respect to the sovereignty of countries and international law, adding that wars "never solve problems."

  [^cite_note-capeverde-309]

  &#x20;The Ministry of Economy warned about the impact on business and that the country counts with the support of Portugal and other countries from the UE in case of requiring assistance.

  [^cite_note-capeverde-309]

  &#x20;Cape Verde later cosponsored [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2817](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2817), condemning Iran's retaliatory attacks on the Gulf states.

  [^cite_note-\:0-305]

-  [Central African Republic](5478.md): The Central African Republic cosponsored [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2817](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2817), condemning Iran's retaliatory attacks on the Gulf states.

  [^cite_note-\:0-305]

- [Chad](5488.md) [Chad](5488.md): President [Mahamat Déby](39073394.md) expressed sympathy for Iran following the attack by Israel and the United States and offered his condolences to the Islamic Republic of Iran following the announcement of the death of its supreme leader. He said that he was sending his "most sincere and heartfelt condolences" for the killing of Khamenei and that he shared "the pain of the Iranian nation in the face of this difficult ordeal".

  [^cite_note-Jazeera-249]

  &#x20;Chad later cosponsored [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2817](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2817), condemning Iran's retaliatory attacks on the Gulf states.

  [^cite_note-\:0-305]

-  [Comoros](5403.md): The Comoros cosponsored [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2817](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2817), condemning Iran's retaliatory attacks on the Gulf states.

  [^cite_note-\:0-305]

-  [Djibouti](17207794.md): The [Ministry of Foreign Affairs and International Cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_and_International_Cooperation_\(Djibouti\)) condemned Iran's retaliatory attacks in territory of the [Gulf states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_states_\(Middle_East\)), arguing that such states called for peace and were not seeking war, urging for an immediate de-escalation and a return to [dialogue](79685207.md).

  [^cite_note-310]

  &#x20;Shortly after the president [Ismaïl Omar Guelleh](392341.md), while reaffirming alignment with Saudi Arabia by granting them access to military facilities, warned Somaliland about the possibility of establishing an Israeli base in [Berbera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berbera) saying that would threaten regional stability across the Horn of Africa and the neutrality of the Red Sea countries in the [Israel-Houthi conflict](75207766.md), accusing both Israel and the UAE of driving strategic realignments across Northeast Africa that intensify the risk of current conflicts (like the [Somali Civil War](3070332.md), [Sudanese Civil War](73556699.md), [Insurgency in Chad](67450724.md), [Libyan crisis](44791215.md)) and possibly merging them with the [Yemeni civil war](46215853.md) and the 2026 Iran war.

  [^cite_note-Jazeera-249]

  &#x20;Djibouti later cosponsored [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2817](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2817), condemning Iran's retaliatory attacks on the Gulf states.

  [^cite_note-\:0-305]

  &#x20;Gambia later cosponsored [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2817](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2817), condemning Iran's retaliatory attacks on the Gulf states.

  [^cite_note-\:0-305]

  [^cite_note-fourafricannations-304]

  &#x20;Liberia later cosponsored [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2817](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2817), condemning Iran's retaliatory attacks on the Gulf states.

-  [Libya](17633.md): Libya's foreign ministry condemned both the U.S.–Israeli strikes on Iran and the Iranian retaliatory strikes on several Arab states, stating that they constitute a breach of the principles of good neighborliness and respect for state sovereignty as stipulated in international law.

  [^cite_note-314]

  [^cite_note-315]

  &#x20;The royal cabinet released a statement saying that King [Mohammed VI](226113.md) conversed with the leaders of the United Arab Emirates, Bahrain, Qatar and Saudi Arabia by telephone to express his condemnation.

  [^cite_note-317]

  [^cite_note-318]

  [^cite_note-\:0-305]
